--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insight from Round 2:** The champion jumped massively from 12436 to 3012! The winning formula is extremely simple:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue/3) → transitions back to itself on BLACK floor (p=0.95) or tiny random probability (0.01)
- State 1: Attraction → transitions back to State 0 on GRAY floor (p=0.8) or when few neighbors (below 4, w=10)

The logic: robots go toward blue walls (target strip). When they reach black floor, they stay in GoToColor (confirming they're in the right strip). When on gray (between strips), they switch to Attraction to pull toward group. The "few neighbors" condition in state 1 sends them back to GoToColor if isolated.

**Second place (3297)** adds more transitions but is slightly worse - the simplicity of the champion is key.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion verbatim
2. Exploit: tweak att gain, floor transition probabilities, neighbor threshold
3. Explore: try 3-state variants adding a "stay on black" state, different attraction parameters, GoAwayColor(green) variants

Key parameters to tune:
- att1: try 2.5, 3.5, 4.0
- p1x0 (gray→GoToColor): try 0.7, 0.9
- p1x1 (few neighbors threshold): try 3, 5, 6
- w1x1: try 8, 15
- p0x0 (black→stay): try 0.99, 0.9, 0.8

## Reasoning

**Observations from Round 3:**
- The champion (3012) survived unchanged. Almost all top machines are 2-state variants of the same topology.
- The design space around the champion is very tight: scores 3073-3317 for the top 10, then a big gap to 4684+.
- 2-state topology dominates. 3-state variants perform worse.
- Key parameters for the champion:
  - State 0 (GoToColor blue): BLACK floor → stay (p=0.95), random tiny (0.01)
  - State 1 (Attraction): GRAY → return to GoToColor (0.8), few neighbors (<4, w=10) → return (p=4 threshold)

**What to try:**
- The top scorer in round 3 used `p1x1=5, w1x1=15` (threshold=5, steepness=15) - slightly higher neighbor threshold
- Second place used 3 transitions in state 1 with black-floor condition (p=0.9)
- The att gain of 2.5 performed well (rank 3)
- Mostly fine-tune: neighbor threshold (3-6), steepness (8-20), att gain (2.0-4.0), gray probability (0.7-0.9), black probability in state 0 (0.9-0.99)

**Plan:**
1. Champion verbatim (elitism)
2. Variants with p1x1=5,6,7 and w1x1=12,15,18,20
3. Variants with att=2.0, 2.5, 3.0, 3.5, 4.0
4. Variants combining the best features (p1x0=0.8 or 0.9, att=2.5-3.0, threshold=5)
5. One or two exploration attempts with slightly different structures

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 4:**
The champion stays at 3012. All 14 machines scored between 3062-3522, a very tight band. The topology is completely converged: 2-state GoToColor(blue) → Attraction with black/random transitions out of state 0, and gray/few-neighbors transitions out of state 1.

**Key parameter insights:**
- `p1x1=6, w1x1=18` (rank 2, 3080) slightly better than `p1x1=5, w1x1=15` (rank 3-4)
- `p1x1=4, w1x1=10` (champion) remains best ever
- `p0x0=0.97, p0x1=0.005, att=2.5, p1x0=0.85, p1x1=5, w1x1=12` scored well (rank 5)
- 3-transition variants in state 1 adding black-floor condition are competitive but not better
- Extra transition in state 0 (perceive blue color) hurts (rank 14)

**Fine-tuning focus:**
- Try p1x1=7, w1x1=20 (push higher threshold)
- Try p1x1=4, w1x1=15 (sharper sigmoid at same threshold)
- Try p1x1=4, w1x1=8 (softer)
- Try w1x1=20 with threshold 6
- Try p0x0=0.98, 0.96
- Try att=3.0 with p1x0=0.85
- Try p0x1=0.015
- Some exploration: try GoAwayColor(green=2) instead, or different color indices

**Analysis of Round 6:**
- New champion: 2970.69 with `p0x0=0.97, p0x1=0.005, att=3, p1x0=0.85, p1x1=6, w1x1=18`
- Three machines tied at exactly 2970.69 (ranks 1-3), differing only in `w1x1` (18 vs 15) and `att1` (3 vs 3.5)
- The parameter sweet spot is very clear:
  - `p0x0=0.97` (not 0.96, not 0.98)
  - `p0x1=0.005` (not 0.003, not 0.01)
  - `att1=3.0` or `3.5` both work
  - `p1x0=0.85` (not 0.8, not 0.9)
  - `p1x1=6` threshold (not 4-5, not 7)
  - `w1x1=15-18` (both tied at best score)

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 7:**
- Champion unchanged at 2970.69
- Best this round: 2972.98 with `p0x1=0.006` (vs champion's 0.005)
- Second: 2977.02 with `p0x1=0.004`
- Many tied at 2990.53 — the core parameters `p0x0=0.97, p0x1=0.005, p1x0=0.85, p1x1=6` with various `w1x1` (16,18,20) and `att1` (3.0,3.2,3.5) all tied
- `p1x0=0.85` is optimal (0.83 worse, 0.87 worse)
- `p0x0=0.975` is worse than 0.97
- `att1=4.0` is much worse (3309)
- `p1x1=7` slightly worse than 6; `p1x1=5` much worse

**What's left to tune:**
- `p0x1`: 0.004-0.006 is the sweet spot; try 0.0045, 0.0055, 0.007
- `p0x0`: 0.97 seems optimal; try 0.969, 0.971
- `p1x0`: 0.85 optimal; try 0.84, 0.86
- `w1x1`: 16-20 all tie; try 14
- `att1`: 3.0-3.5 all tie; locked in
- Maybe try `p1x1=6` with `w1x1=17`
- Add some exploration: slightly different topologies

The scores are converging tightly around 2970-3000. The stochasticity of the simulator means small differences may not be meaningful. Let me focus on the most promising micro-tuning and add some diversity.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 8:**
- Champion unchanged at 2970.69 — no improvement this round despite many near-identical variants
- Round 8 was notably worse overall (3104-3314) compared to round 7 (2972-3309) — this is simulation stochasticity
- The champion parameters `p0x0=0.97, p0x1=0.005, att=3, p1x0=0.85, p1x1=6, w1x1=18` remain the best found
- Micro-tuning around `p0x1` (0.004-0.007) and `w1x1` (14-20) shows no consistent improvement - the differences are within simulation noise
- We are stuck in a local optimum with this 2-state topology

**Key insight:** We have been over-exploiting the same parameter neighborhood for 4+ rounds without improvement. We need genuine structural exploration while keeping the champion.

**What to try:**
1. Champion verbatim (elitism)
2. A few near-champion variants (p0x1=0.005-0.007, w1x1=16-20)
3. **Real exploration**: Try completely different approaches:
   - GoAwayColor(green=2): robots flee green walls → end up in blue strip
   - 3-state with GoToColor + Stop when aggregated on black
   - FlockCenter as secondary behavior
   - Different color: maybe clr=6 (cyan) or other colors for blue walls
   - Try GoToColor staying in state on black floor, but use neighbor attraction more aggressively

Note: The scores in this topology seem to bottom out around 2970. Let me try some genuinely different structures.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
